# Skills · P · part 7

856 entries, pair to palaia-capture. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **pair**: Pair computers in the Agent Fleet with a one-time code, without copying connection.json. On a worker it opens a 10-minute pairing window and shows the code; on an orchestrator it searches the local network and Tailscale for workers waiting to pair and connects with the code the user gives. Use when the user invokes /pair, or asks to pair, connect or add a computer, get a new pairing code, or says a pairing code expired — e.g. "生成配对码", "搜索并配对新电脑，配对码 2726-9703", "pair with 192.168.1.23", "connect my other PC". ([cxfydhcg/agent-fleet](https://github.com/cxfydhcg/agent-fleet/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/pair))
- **pair**: Surface the human as the pair voice while the sidecar mechanism is being chosen. Reminds the pilot that the four pair objections (missing test, oversized batch, divergence-age, mixed concerns) will come from the human in chat until a sidecar agent or hook-emitted prompt is wired up. ([davewil/oh-my-tbd](https://github.com/davewil/oh-my-tbd/tree/HEAD/skills/pair))
- **pair**: Spike-driven pairing mode — the user authors all load-bearing code by hand in their IDE; Claude navigates the codebase, fences behavior with tests, and executes only explicitly named transformations. Use when the user invokes /pair at the start of a coding session. ([dwb/hm](https://github.com/dwb/hm/tree/HEAD/conf/claude/skills/pair))
- **pair**: Use when a long or autonomous coding/agent session could drift off-spec and you want a fresh-context second model supervising the primary agent and nudging it back. Triggers (intent-bearing phrases, not single words): "pair a watcher with my session", "supervise this run", "keep the agent on track", "catch drift" / "guard against drift", "is the agent off-spec", "babysit my agent", "re-anchor me to the spec", "watch the watcher", "mwm", or any request to set up / launch the pair watcher harness. NOT for one-off "watch this file/build/deploy" requests (that is loop/agent-browser/verify). ([GarrettMooney/dotfiles](https://github.com/GarrettMooney/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/claude/skills/pair))

- **pair**: Pair-programming judgement gate. Run it before asking your pair a question and before saying a task is done. ([jagreehal/shepherd](https://github.com/jagreehal/shepherd/tree/HEAD/skills/pair))

- **pair**: Plan, build and review protocol for two Claude Code sessions (ideally running different models). They coordinate through a markdown thread file and a design doc with a Decision Log, so the user approves decisions instead of carrying messages between terminals. Modes are plan &lt;topic&gt; · build &lt;thread&gt; · review &lt;thread&gt; · answer &lt;thread&gt; · status. The user writes the spine of the code (the logic and decisions) by hand over a traced plan, the boilerplate is filled in for them, each step is checked by running it, and every decision is remembered. How much the user types and how much is explained follows their knowledge level in each technology, from 0 (every word explained) to 10 (the user decides and Senior types). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-fastq-quality-control))
- **paired-end-fastq-read-handling**: Use when input data are gzip-compressed paired-end FASTQ short reads (e.g., the SRR15859208 E. coli NDM5 dataset) and a k-mer distribution or Bloom filter input is needed before k/b selection for ntEdit+Sealer. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-fastq-read-handling))
- **paired-end-fragment-extraction**: Use when STAR-aligned paired-end scRNA-seq BAMs (e.g., Smart-seq2 data like gray mouse lemur testes cells) must be converted to strand-specific interval fragments before groHMM TAR discovery, because groHMM consumes read coverage intervals rather than raw BAM alignments. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-fragment-extraction))
- **paired-end-illumina-read-simulation**: Use when building synthetic FASTQ benchmarks from known viral reference genomes (e.g. BASV, BKV, HIV-1) to evaluate contig extension or assembly accuracy under varied read length, error rate, and depth conditions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-illumina-read-simulation))
- **paired-end-insert-size-distribution-filtering**: Use when profiling metagenomic read mappings against reference genomes and needing to discard improperly paired or aberrant-insert-size reads before computing coverage, ANI, or SNVs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-insert-size-distribution-filtering))
- **paired-end-metagenomic-read-preprocessing**: Use when reconstructing full-length SSU rRNA genes (16S/18S) from paired-end Illumina metagenomic FASTQ to enable species-level taxonomic profiling via RiboTaxa's dual-assembler strategy. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-metagenomic-read-preprocessing))
- **paired-end-naming-convention-application**: Reference for setting up a NEAT project with paired-end sequencing data where reverse-read FASTQ files must be linked to their forward mates inside the centralized Targets.txt file. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-naming-convention-application))
- **paired-end-overlap-analysis**: Use when assessing duplication levels of high-depth (e.g. ~10000x cfDNA) paired-end Illumina FASTQ data, or when deciding whether a tool's duplication metric accounts for read1/read2 pairing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-analysis))
- **paired-end-overlap-graph-assembly**: Use when you have a FASTA of mixed single- and paired-end viral reads (e.g. TAR-VIR recruited_reads.fa) and need to reconstruct strain-level haplotypes de novo, without a complete reference genome. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-graph-assembly))
- **paired-end-overlap-match-probability-computation**: Use when scoring overlapping paired-end FASTQ reads (e.g. 108-base Illumina reads) to decide which assembled sequences pass a quality threshold such as p = 0.9 or p = 0.6. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-match-probability-computation))
- **paired-end-overlap-scoring**: Use when merging overlapping paired-end Illumina FASTQ reads (e.g. 16S rRNA amplicons) where naive perfect-match overlap fails due to uncalled (N) or miscalled bases, and error-correcting probabilistic assembly is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-overlap-scoring))
- **paired-end-read-alignment**: Use when metagenomic FASTQ (paired-end WGSIM-simulated or real shotgun) reads must be mapped to a bacterial reference set to produce unique/split read BAMs for HGT breakpoint detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment))
- **paired-end-read-alignment-bwa**: Use when running Anchored-fusion or its benchmark against de novo fusion callers: paired-end FASTQ (R1/R2) must be mapped with BWA-MEM (&gt;=0.7.17) to a target-gene fasta and genome fa (hg38/hg19) with matching GTF. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment-bwa))
- **paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference**: Use when paired-end RIMS-seq FASTQ reads must be aligned to a reference genome — either user-supplied (option 2) or de novo assembled with SPAdes (option 1) — as a prerequisite for methylase specificity discovery. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference))
- **paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference-genome**: Use when synthetic scRNA-seq reads exist as unaligned FASTQ files (e.g., minnow output) and must be placed in genomic coordinates on mm10 to compare coverage against an aligned real BAM. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-alignment-to-reference-genome))
- **paired-end-read-assembly**: Use when Illumina paired-end FASTQ (or SAM/BAM) amplicon reads overlap and must be merged into full-length sequences with mismatch/uncalled-base correction, e.g., 16S rRNA libraries where naive assembly discards ~40% of reads. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-assembly--4aace21ef51e1d92))
- **paired-end-read-assembly**: Use when raw paired-end Illumina reads (e.g. SRA FASTQ like ERR008613) must be converted into genome assembly contig FASTAs for comparison with QUAST/WebQUAST. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-assembly--a5035386db5a753c))
- **paired-end-read-group-organization**: Use when a multi-sample sequencing workflow must ingest raw FASTQ inputs from a sample sheet into per-read-group QC and trimming steps, distinguishing paired-end (R1/R2) from single-end files for DNA-seq and RNA-seq. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-group-organization))
- **paired-end-read-local-reassembly**: Use when a draft scaffold FASTA contains N-wildcard gap regions between contigs and a paired-end short-read dataset (e.g. Illumina GAGE PE reads) is available to fill those gaps by local reassembly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-local-reassembly))
- **paired-end-read-mapping-to-multi-genome-reference**: Use when a metagenomic analysis needs paired-end shotgun reads mapped against many reference genomes simultaneously so junction read pairs spanning two different references can seed donor/receptor detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-mapping-to-multi-genome-reference))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when starting from per-sample paired-end gzipped FASTQ amplicon reads (e.g. Sample1_1.fq.gz/Sample1_2.fq.gz) and you need merged, primer-trimmed, quality-filtered amplicons before dereplication and OTU/ASV inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--ab07c7402e44b94f))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when raw paired-end amplicon FASTQ reads have been demultiplexed into per-sample files and must be merged and quality-filtered before OTU clustering or ASV denoising. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--ab17ce572e203e05))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when raw paired-end FASTQ reads from stool metatranscriptomes must be joined into longer single fragments (longer than 100 bp) before annotation, as the SAMSA pipeline aligns paired-end files after Trimmomatic cleaning. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--063adc5fe8d7694c))
- **paired-end-read-merging**: Use when raw paired-end metatranscriptome RNA-seq data in FASTQ form must be converted into a single merged, trimmed, rRNA-filtered FASTQ before DIAMOND annotation, as in the SAMSA2 preprocessing step. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-merging--a2cb7b274000643e))
- **paired-end-read-overlap-assembly**: Use when processing paired-end, 3'-end tag scRNA-seq data (e.g. Microwell-seq FASTQ from Mouse Cell Atlas / Human Cell Landscape) where reads must overlap and be merged to recover the full insert containing the 3' cleavage site. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-overlap-assembly))
- **paired-end-read-pair-categorization**: Use when raw paired-end ChIA-PET (or HiChIP/PLAC-seq) FASTQ reads must be scanned for the bridge linker junction and partitioned by linker/tag content before read mapping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-read-pair-categorization))
- **paired-end-read-pair-integrity-checking**: Use when a paired-end QC/trimming step (e.g. fastp) has produced cleaned_1.fastq.gz and cleaned_2.fastq.gz and you must confirm the pair is synchronized, non-empty, and valid gzip FASTQ before hybrid assembly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-pair-integrity-checking))
- **paired-end-read-pairing-handling**: Use when classifying paired-end FASTQ read pairs (e.g. simulated 2x250 nt Illumina reads) with Kaiju, or when benchmarking throughput where read-pair ordering and naming conventions must be preserved. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-pairing-handling))
- **paired-end-read-primer-trimming**: Use when raw paired-end amplicon FASTQs (e.g., dual-indexed 181 bp COI reads from SRA PRJNA518082 Libraries A-D) still carry primer sequences and must be trimmed before denoising or OTU clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-primer-trimming))
- **paired-end-read-quality-trimming**: Use when raw paired-end FASTQ reads (e.g. from SRA accessions like ERR008613) must be quality-checked and low-quality ends removed before assembly, with surviving mate pairs kept as FASTQ.GZ. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-quality-trimming))
- **paired-end-read-quantification**: Use when you have paired-end RNA-seq reads (FASTQ) and a reference transcriptome (FASTA), and you need to estimate transcript-level expression (NumReads and TPM). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v1/skills/paired-end-read-quantification))
- **paired-end-read-separate-clustering**: Use when processing paired-end 16S FASTQ reads with highly overlapping mates where quality drops in the overlap region make read merging lossy, and you want annotated OTUs without a merging step. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-separate-clustering))
- **paired-end-read-simulation-to-single-end**: Use when deciding whether paired-end sequencing is necessary for metatranscriptome annotation, or when quantifying how much annotation information is lost by processing paired-end reads as single-end. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-read-simulation-to-single-end))
- **paired-end-reverse-complement-handling**: Use when classifying paired-end reads (e.g., setB2_1/setB2_2 FASTA files) whose strand orientation is unknown, and classification quality depends on considering forward and reverse-complement strands. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-reverse-complement-handling))
- **paired-end-rna-seq-alignment**: Use when performing a targeted fusion search on paired-end RNA-seq FASTQ data by anchoring a gene of interest, and reads must be aligned to both the target gene transcript fasta and a whole-genome reference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-rna-seq-alignment))
- **paired-end-short-read-alignment**: Use when you have paired-end FASTQ files (e.g. in.R1.fq.gz/in.R2.fq.gz) that must be mapped to a reference genome (hg19) and compared on alignment quality metrics across preprocessing variants. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/paired-end-short-read-alignment))
- **paired-end-similarity-matrix-computation**: Use when classifying paired-end metagenomic reads with LiME and you must build similarity matrices for all four collections (Reads1F, Reads1RC, Reads2F, Reads2RC) merged against the reference genomes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-similarity-matrix-computation))
- **paired-end-splicing-label-derivation**: Use when building an evaluation read set with definitive splicing ground truth from paired-end 10X scRNA-seq data (e.g., mouse/human scRNA-seq) to benchmark splicing-status predictors via AUC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-splicing-label-derivation))
- **paired-end-strand-and-mate-handling**: Use when classifying paired-end metagenomic reads (e.g. 100 bp Illumina mates from SRR1804065 or setB2) with LiME, when strand direction is unknown and ambiguous reads must be resolved with mate information. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-strand-and-mate-handling))
- **paired-end-structural-variant-calling**: Use when analyzing sorted/indexed Illumina paired-end BAM/CRAM alignments (e.g. bwa mem-aligned HG002 WGS at ~20-40x) to detect SVs/indels ≥30 bp, or when benchmarking PE SV callers against a GIAB truth set with svbench. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-structural-variant-calling))
- **paired-end-support-threshold-filtering**: Use when a consensus VCF of SV calls (merged across callers with SURVIVOR) must be down-filtered before manual curation — e.g. dropping calls with low paired-end support or breakpoints in low-complexity or annotated LTR regions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/paired-end-support-threshold-filtering))
- **paired-end-tag-alignment-and-bedpe-construction**: Use when processing raw ChIA-PET paired-end FASTQ data after linker trimming and you need aligned PETs as deduplicated BEDPE pairs for downstream peak and loop calling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-tag-alignment-and-bedpe-construction))
- **paired-end-tag-fraction-computation**: Use when processing ChIA-PET fastq data through ChIA-PET2 and you need to quantify per-step PET retention (pre-/post-alignment, post-duplicate-removal, intra-chromosomal &gt;=4 PETs) for quality control. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-end-tag-fraction-computation))
- **paired-evaluation-execution**: Prepare or audit paired evaluate-gsf trials with frozen common inputs, independent evaluator pins, complete attempt accounting and separately authorized native execution. ([wfcosta/SoftwareFactory](https://github.com/wfcosta/SoftwareFactory/tree/HEAD/.gsf/skills/paired-evaluation-execution) · ★6)
- **paired-field-mismatch-layout**: Keep side-by-side compact form fields visually equal when only one shows mismatch, without adding row height ([yashasg/knitting-gauge-reconciler](https://github.com/yashasg/knitting-gauge-reconciler/tree/HEAD/.squad/skills/paired-field-mismatch-layout))
- **paired-flag-multi-instance-registration**: CLI pattern for registering N instances of a composite config (e.g. spec+baseUrl pairs) using repeated paired flags and a pending-state parser. ([kjuhwa/skills-hub](https://github.com/kjuhwa/skills-hub/tree/HEAD/skills/cli/paired-flag-multi-instance-registration))
- **paired-flotation-water-route**: Create original short videos using the 双脚浮具水面路线｜交替承重、连续尾流与远端抵达 Creative DNA for MiniMax H3 or Seedance 2.0. Use when the user wants this causal, camera, motion, rhythm, or payoff structure with new subjects and surfaces. ([JGRFW/comfyui-AICG3D-SKILLS](https://github.com/JGRFW/comfyui-AICG3D-SKILLS/tree/HEAD/skills/paired-flotation-water-route) · ★165 · 2 copies)
- **paired-hardware-lifecycle-contract**: **Domain:** System architecture / device integration **Applies to:** Any project that pairs a host (phone, watch, laptop) with a peripheral (BLE accessory, USB device, HID controller, AR/VR display, hearing aid, dock). **Origin:** AR-Runner ADR 2026-05-19 — "BLE link is user-managed, not workout-scoped." ([jkrilov/AR-Runner](https://github.com/jkrilov/AR-Runner/tree/HEAD/.squad/skills/paired-hardware-lifecycle-contract))
- **paired-image-graph-training-data-preparation**: Use when preparing training data for GILoop: the input is a KR-normalized intra-chromosomal Hi-C contact map (or Juicer-dump O/E matrix) and paired image and graph samples are needed for dual-branch loop-calling training. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/paired-image-graph-training-data-preparation))
- **paired-inference**: Route Pix2Pix-Turbo paired image-translation inference for edge-to-image, stochastic sketch-to-image, custom Pix2Pix checkpoints, and local paired Gradio demos. ([VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill](https://github.com/VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/repositories/repo-skills/img2img-turbo/sub-skills/paired-inference) · ★357)
- **paired-insertion-counting-strategy**: Use when you have loaded fragment data from single-cell ATAC-seq experiments into a backed AnnData object (with fragments stored in .obsm['fragment_paired'] or . ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v1/skills/paired-insertion-counting-strategy))
- **paired-inventory**: Paired V1/V2 lane inventory: the same keyed rows from both trees, set-diffed into candidates — read-only. ([adnanmokhtar/refract](https://github.com/adnanmokhtar/refract/tree/HEAD/templates/packs/migration/skills/paired-inventory) · ★2)
- **paired-model-comparison-significance-testing**: Use when comparing a new causal-inference or prediction model against baseline configurations on the same candidate peak-gene pairs, and you need per-configuration retrieval AUROCs plus significance statistics for the differences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-model-comparison-significance-testing))
- **paired-multiomics-data-preprocessing**: Use when you have raw paired scRNA-seq/scATAC-seq count matrices (AnnData objects) that must be normalized and feature-processed before training or evaluating a scButterfly cross-modality translation model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-multiomics-data-preprocessing))
- **paired-multiomics-tnse-visualization**: Use when you need qualitative visual confirmation that translated single-cell profiles (predicted scRNA-seq or scATAC-seq from a cross-modality translator) cluster by cell type and match true labeled profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-multiomics-tnse-visualization))
- **paired-nonparametric-hypothesis-testing**: Use when two configurations of the same method (e.g. weighted vs unweighted decoupleR runs) yield paired scores over identical dataset/regulator combinations and a directional significance claim must be tested nonparametrically. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/paired-nonparametric-hypothesis-testing))
- **paired-nonparametric-method-comparison**: Use when two or more metabolomics normalization methods must be compared on identical GC-MS feature-intensity data with paired per-metabolite precision (RSD) values, to decide which method yields the highest coverage of compounds passing a &lt;30% RSD threshold. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-nonparametric-method-comparison))
- **paired-observation-alignment**: Use when two score vectors cover the same methods/entities measured under different conditions or modalities and you must drop methods missing in either modality before computing a paired correlation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/paired-observation-alignment))
- **paired-omics-bgc-metabolite-linking-workflow**: Use when you have paired genomic and metabolomic data from the same microbial strains and want to link biosynthetic gene clusters (BGCs) to the metabolite features they plausibly encode — mine BGCs from assembled genomes with antiSMASH, tokenize BGC domains with iPRESTO/Pfam and cluster them into gene cluster families (GCFs) with BiG-SCAPE, build an MS/MS molecular network from paired LC-MS/MS data with GNPS to obtain molecular families (MFs), then score GCF-MF co-occurrence across strains (NPLinker-style Metcalf/hypergeometric scoring) to produce a ranked table of candidate BGC-metabolite li… ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/workflows/paired-omics-bgc-metabolite-linking) · 2 copies)
- **paired-omics-bgc-spectrum-link-validation**: Use when a paired omics (genome + metabolomics) dataset contains curated validated genome-metabolome links and you must quantify how many survive Metcalf and NPClassScore filtering, or verify prioritisation/rank improvements of known links. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-bgc-spectrum-link-validation))
- **paired-omics-data-retrieval**: Use when a linking or benchmarking analysis needs paired genome+LC-MS/MS samples with validated BGC–metabolite links, e.g. from the PoDP or a GNPS job task, to build fingerprints or evaluate precision/recall. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-data-retrieval))
- **paired-omics-data-structure-mapping**: Use when when you have a paired omics project document (JSON format) that combines MS/MS mass spectrometry data with genome identifiers, biosynthetic gene cluster information, sample preparation, extraction method, and instrumentation metadata, and you need to verify it conforms to the platform's. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/paired-omics-data-structure-mapping) · 2 copies)
- **paired-omics-dataset-configuration**: Use when a paired genome–metabolome dataset with a Paired Omics Data Platform (PoDP) accession (e.g. MSV000078836, MSV000084950, MSV000084771) must be assembled in NPLinker for BGC–MS/MS spectrum linking. - **paired-omics-dataset-curation**: Use when building a genome-mining workflow that needs matched genome (or metagenome) and metabolome data for the same samples, e.g., to train or query NPOmix against PoDP paired datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-dataset-curation))
- **paired-omics-fingerprint-vector-analysis**: Use when you have paired genome/metagenome + LC-MS/MS samples and need to link MS/MS spectra (GNPS reference or cryptic) to biosynthetic gene clusters with confidence scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/paired-omics-fingerprint-vector-analysis))
- **paired-omics-metadata-retrieval**: Use when an analysis needs systematically curated BGC–metabolite linkages: e.g. assembling validated paired datasets for NPLinker scoring, benchmarking link-ranking algorithms, or finding projects by genus, extraction method, or metadata ontology terms. - **paired-omics-precision-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when NPOmix KNN predictions (GCF labels assigned to query MS/MS spectra) must be scored against validated BGC-metabolite links from MIBiG/PoDP to report top-1 and top-10 precision. - **paired-omics-project-filtering**: Use when the analysis goal is to find PoDP projects by extraction or sample-preparation metadata (e.g. projects that used methanol to extract cell pellets) via keyword search over project documents. - **paired-probe**: Refuses a verdict a check did not earn: runs it with the fault present and absent and blocks when both agree; catches zero-sample sweeps and swallowed errors. Trigger when user explicitly requests paired programming or when working on complex/novel features requiring collaboration. ([matthew-bram/enterprise-confluent-test-probe](https://github.com/matthew-bram/enterprise-confluent-test-probe/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/paired-programming))

- **paired-read-concatenation-for-classification**: Use when classifying paired-end reads from simulated or real metagenomes (e.g., HiSeq, MiSeq or simBA-5 read sets) with a k-mer LCA classifier that accepts single sequences, and you want both mates of a pair to vote together for one taxon. - **paired-read-merging-with-overlap-detection**: Use when paired-end Illumina read pairs overlap and must be converted into single merged fragments before downstream k-mer counting or seed construction, e.g. to derive 'not too repetitive' seeds for TULIP. - **paired-replicate-statistical-model-fitting**: Use when you have per-allele exon inclusion (IJC) and skipping (SJC) counts plus effective isoform lengths (incLen, skpLen) from replicate RNA-seq samples sharing a heterozygous SNP, and need to test for differential splicing between alleles across replicates. - **paired-reversal-cancellation**: Reference for paired-reversal handling on GL batch tapes: an RV row whose ref-trace points at an earlier row cancels BOTH legs (the RV and the row it references) — but ONLY when the two value-dates fall within the shop's reversal settlement window; an RV that references a too-old posting is NOT a cancellation and posts one-sided. - **paired-statistical-significance-testing**: Use when two or more methods have been evaluated on the same set of datasets and a per-dataset paired comparison of scores (e.g., AMI/NMI/ARI) must establish statistically significant ity rather than just a higher mean rank. - **paired-strain-differential-expression**: Use when you have expression data from strains that carry a duplication paired with a non-duplicated strain of the same clonal population, and you need to test whether duplicated-region genes are induced and whether induction tracks copy number. - **paired-t-test-replicate-statistics**: Use when eCLIP/skipper reproducible_enriched_windows tables exist for matched WT and mutant replicates and you need to quantify region-proportion shifts (CDS, deep introns) or test differential enrichment (e.g., tRNA loci) with replicate-level statistics. - **paired-t-test-significance-testing**: Use when comparing the clustering performance (AMI) of two methods evaluated on the same matched set of datasets or simulation replicates — e.g., 12 paired section-level AMI values — and you need a p-value for whether the improvement is statistically significant. - **paired-t-test-statistical-testing**: Use when two classification conditions (e.g., species-level with bespoke weights vs. genus-level with uniform weights) have been evaluated on the same matched test samples and you must test whether per-sample error rates or Bray-Curtis dissimilarities differ significantly. - **paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing**: Use when quantifying real-time depletion effects (e.g., RISER globin mRNA depletion in whole blood) and a null-comparison is needed against per-transcript percent changes arising from random splitting of an independent control run. - **paired-wilcoxon-significance-testing**: Use when comparing paired per-fold performance metrics (Accuracy or macro F1-Score) of a MSMCE-integrated model vs. its baseline from 6-fold stratified cross-validation to establish statistical significance. - **pairwise-contrast-pvalue-computation**: Use when you have a wide-format feature table (features as rows, samples as columns) plus a design file mapping samples to treatment groups, and you need per-feature pairwise group contrast p-values and binary significance flags. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-contrast-pvalue-computation))
- **pairwise-correlation-computation**: Use when you have a processed samples-by-metabolites table (post Viime imputation/normalization/transformation/scaling) and need to explore correlated behavior of metabolites, e.g. to build a metabolite network diagram whose edges encode signed correlation strength. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-correlation-computation))
- **pairwise-daily-briefing**: Give a Pairwise user a short briefing on what needs their attention: open service requests and their progress, and recent feedback, offers and team changes from their notifications. Use when the user asks "what's new in Pairwise?", "what needs my attention today?", or wants a morning or weekly hiring summary. Follows the pairwise-mcp skill for words and links. ([Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills](https://github.com/Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-daily-briefing) · ★4)
- **pairwise-dam-overlap-analysis**: Use when several normalization methods have been applied to the same metabolomics dataset and you need to quantify consistency of their differential abundance calls (e.g. DAM overlap and p-value similarity across methods). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-dam-overlap-analysis))
- **pairwise-delivery-client-roles-summary**: Summarize the open service requests a recruiting team is working on for its clients: for each client, every role's priority, location, recent activity and candidates in evaluation, with quiet roles flagged and an optional nudge message for the recruiting team. Use when a delivery, account or recruiting manager asks "how are we doing on &lt;client&gt;'s roles?", "which roles need attention?", "which roles have gone quiet?", or wants a client roles report. Follows the pairwise-mcp skill. ([Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills](https://github.com/Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-delivery-client-roles-summary) · ★4)
- **pairwise-design-judging**: Judge competing UI and design candidates with blind, order-swapped pairwise comparisons and a Bradley–Terry fit instead of absolute 0–10 scores. Use when running design tournaments or iterative improve-and-judge loops with sub-agent designers and judges, when absolute judge scores saturate or tie, or when judges from different models score with different biases. ([ttulttul/pairwise-design-judging](https://github.com/ttulttul/pairwise-design-judging/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-design-judging))
- **pairwise-differential-gene-expression-analysis**: Use when a SingleCellExperiment of CD8 T cells has been assigned to reference states and you need gene signatures per state pair (or per state) to score/query cells or train a state classifier. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-differential-gene-expression-analysis))
- **pairwise-distance-correlation-analysis**: Use when a pseudo-space has been reconstructed from scRNA-seq data using an ST reference (e.g., 10X Visium DLPFC slice 151674) and the geometric fidelity of the reconstruction must be quantified before downstream interpretation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-distance-correlation-analysis))
- **pairwise-distance-correlation-evaluation**: Use when evaluating recovered 2D cell/spot coordinates from scRNA-seq against a spatial transcriptomics reference (Visium, MERSCOPE, MERFISH) where true test-set coordinates are known and methods like CeLEry, Tangram, spaOTsc, and novoSpaRc are being compared. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-distance-correlation-evaluation))
- **pairwise-distance-matrix-construction**: Use when you have processed MS/MS spectra per LC-MS/MS feature and need a square pairwise spectral-similarity matrix as input for k-medoids clustering or feature-set testing in msFeaST. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-distance-matrix-construction))
- **pairwise-distance-matrix-construction**: Use when you have a normalized scRNA-seq matrix with cell-type, condition, and patient metadata and need a cell-type-by-cell-type distance matrix (e.g., to cluster cell types by transcriptomic perturbation magnitude or lineage similarity). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-distance-matrix-construction))
- **pairwise-distance-matrix-tabulation**: Use when you have peak-listed LC-MS/MS (MGF) datasets for multiple samples and need a quantitative pairwise similarity/distance matrix without peptide identification or spectral annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-distance-matrix-tabulation))
- **pairwise-epigenome-local-alignment**: Use when two genomic regions are represented as single-track chromatin state sequences (ChromHMM labels) and you need a local alignment score to find which regions/gene bodies are epigenomically similar to a query region. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-epigenome-local-alignment))
- **pairwise-feature-correlation-analysis**: Use when a Compound Discoverer (v3.3) peak table retains adduct, dimer, or in-source-fragment features co-eluting with identified lipids and pairwise correlation filtering is needed to reduce spurious unidentified peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-feature-correlation-analysis))
- **pairwise-genetic-distance-calculation**: Use when you have FASTA consensus sequences per specimen/marker (e.g. Nanopore rDNA consensus and Illumina reference consensus, or COI barcodes vs rDNA regions) and need a pairwise distance matrix partitioned into intra- vs interspecific classes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-genetic-distance-calculation))
- **pairwise-genome-ani-comparison**: Use when you need sensitive strain-level comparison of microbial populations across metagenomic samples, e.g. testing whether related individuals share more strains than unrelated ones. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-genome-ani-comparison))
- **pairwise-group-comparison-statistics**: Use when a pre-processed (missing-value-handled, log-transformed, pareto-scaled) targeted or untargeted metabolomics feature table must be compared across sample groups to identify features with statistically significant and practically relevant abundance changes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-group-comparison-statistics))
- **pairwise-group-methylation-comparison**: Use when you have a clustered-region methylation counts table (combined.csv with chr, start, end, CpG and per-sample -N/-X count columns) and need to identify DMRs between two or more sample groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-group-methylation-comparison))
- **pairwise-group-significance-testing-of-beta-diversity**: Use when a rarefied OTU/feature table and sample metadata are available and you need an ordination plot plus statistical p-values for every pairwise group comparison of community composition differences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/pairwise-group-significance-testing-of-beta-diversity))
- **pairwise-instrain-profile-comparison**: Use when pairwise genomic comparison of the same genome set across metagenomic samples must distinguish true shared strains from co-occurring but divergent populations, e.g. twin strain-sharing benchmarks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-instrain-profile-comparison))
- **pairwise-interaction-agreement-statistics**: Use when two independent methods (e.g., SMETANA and COMMIT) have predicted metabolite exchanges over the same gap-filled community member models and you need to quantify how much their pairwise interaction predictions agree, including shared metabolites. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-interaction-agreement-statistics))
- **pairwise-interaction-eval**: Evaluates a robot's ability to detect interacting human pairs and classify their coarse-grained interaction types (e.g., walking, standing, sitting together) using bounding box geometry and optical flow, without relying on costly skeleton-based pose estimation. Use when the user wants to benchmark on JRDB, Collective Activity Dataset (CAD), Lawnmower Dataset, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports Accuracy. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/pairwise-interaction-eval) · ★3)
- **pairwise-judge**: Compare two anonymized artifacts under a fixed rubric with fresh swapped-order judgments and explicit ties. ([aman161upadhyay/Calamities-Prediction-Tracking-Alert-System-](https://github.com/aman161upadhyay/Calamities-Prediction-Tracking-Alert-System-/tree/HEAD/reference/ai-skills/03-agents/adversarial-council-agent/skills/pairwise-judge))
- **pairwise-local-alignment-score-computation**: Use when the goal is to score similarity between genomic regions represented as chromatin state sequences (e.g. 500 kb Roadmap query regions vs. other regions or randomized negative controls) with a local alignment algorithm. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-local-alignment-score-computation))
- **pairwise-locus-level-correlation-computation**: Use when evaluating BIRD predictions across multiple cell types: you have predicted and true DH matrices for ≥3 cell types and need pair-wise locus-level accuracy of differential DH, stratified by cell-type similarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-locus-level-correlation-computation))
- **pairwise-ma-methodology**: Deep methodology knowledge for pairwise meta-analysis including fixed vs random effects, heterogeneity assessment, publication bias, and sensitivity analysis. Use when conducting or reviewing pairwise MA. ([choxos/BiostatAgent](https://github.com/choxos/BiostatAgent/tree/HEAD/plugins/itc-modeling/skills/pairwise-ma-methodology) · ★12 · 2 copies)
- **pairwise-mass-comparison-computation**: Use when after peak m/z values and molecular formulas have been extracted and pre-processed from FT-ICR MS data, and you need to reconstruct biochemical transformation networks ab initio. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-mass-comparison-computation))
- **pairwise-mass-difference-computation**: Use when after loading preprocessed MSI intensity data (via msimat from CSV export) or a simple numeric vector of mass peak values, when you need to discover which masses in your dataset co-vary as parent–adduct pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-mass-difference-computation))
- **pairwise-mass-difference-histogram-analysis**: Use when you have peak-mass lists from MALDI-MSI datasets (e.g. SCiLS/MSiReader CSV exports or peakBinned Cardinal imzML) and need to find which peaks are matrix- or salt-adduct partners of parent ions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-mass-difference-histogram-analysis))
- **pairwise-matrix-computation**: Use when when you have a cleaned MS/MS dataset with chemical structure annotations (SMILES, InChI, or InChIKey) and need to generate ground-truth structural similarity labels for training a deep learning model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-matrix-computation) · 2 copies)
- **pairwise-mcp**: Work with Pairwise hiring data through the Pairwise MCP server: service requests (jobs), pipelines, candidates, applications and evaluations (interviews). Covers Pairwise vocabulary and when to use services or internal hiring wording, the order to look things up in, how to link every service request, candidate and evaluation back to its Pairwise page, and what to do when the connection fails. Use for any task that mentions Pairwise, a service request or job, a pipeline, or an evaluation or interview, and before the other pairwise-* skills. ([Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills](https://github.com/Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-mcp) · ★4)
- **pairwise-metabolite-correlation-analysis**: Use when you have a QC-filtered, per-cell anndata object of normalized isotopologue-derived relative uptake fractions and need to test whether uptake of individual fatty acids co-varies across single cells (e.g. control/normoxic liver cancer cells). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-metabolite-correlation-analysis))
- **pairwise-metabolite-overlap-venn-upset-visualization**: Use when you have a filtered FT-ICR MS peak/formula table with assigned molecular formulas and sample-grouping metadata, and you need to quantify and visualize which molecular formulas are shared or unique between user-defined sample groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-metabolite-overlap-venn-upset-visualization))
- **pairwise-must-link-cannot-link-loss-configuration**: Use when clustering scRNA-seq data where purely unsupervised clustering yields biologically uninterpretable clusters and partial prior knowledge (e.g., protein-level ADT distances or known marker subtypes like CD4/CD8) is available for a subset of cell pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-must-link-cannot-link-loss-configuration))
- **pairwise-mz-distance-matrix-construction**: Use when annotating isotope peaks and charge states within grouped LC/MS compound spectra (pseudospectra) from an xsAnnotate/CAMERA peak table, e.g. features from faahKO or MM39-style mixtures where spacing near 1.0033/z must be identified. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-mz-distance-matrix-construction))
- **pairwise-population-xpehh-comparison**: Use when detecting recent selective sweeps by contrasting haplotype lengths between two populations from chromosome-split, tabix-indexed biallelic VCFs plus a sample-to-population .map file. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-population-xpehh-comparison))
- **pairwise-preference-prediction-accuracy**: Measures how well a reward model predicts human preference by comparing its scoring of image pairs against ground-truth human choices. It probes the model's ability to generalize alignment signals to unseen prompts and image distributions. Use when the user has predictions and gold and needs to compute pairwise preference prediction accuracy. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/pairwise-preference-prediction-accuracy) · ★3)
- **pairwise-prioritization**: Prioritize a task list by direct pairwise comparison instead of isolated scoring — compare every pair on importance, urgency, and impact, then produce a ranked execution order. Use when asked 'so từng cặp', 'sắp xếp ưu tiên', 'prioritize these tasks', 'việc nào làm trước', 'rank this backlog', 'đặt độ ưu tiên', or 'compare tasks head to head'. Do NOT use for: choosing between solution options — see option-tournament. Do NOT use for: sprint ceremony planning — see /sprint-planning. ([Horonomos-laps/Yana-AI](https://github.com/Horonomos-laps/Yana-AI/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/pairwise-prioritization) · ★13 · 4 copies)
- **pairwise-ranking**: Select informative comparison pairs, execute judgments, update ratings, and audit coherence until ranking resolution is sufficient. ([yogsoth-ai/de-anthropocentric-research-engine](https://github.com/yogsoth-ai/de-anthropocentric-research-engine/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-ranking) · ★512)
- **pairwise-ranking**: Pairwise Ranking Campaign — produce global rankings through pairwise comparisons and voting aggregation using Bradley-Terry, Elo, TrueSkill, Condorcet, Borda, Schulze methods. ([yogsoth-ai/convergence](https://github.com/yogsoth-ai/convergence/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-ranking) · ★2)
- **pairwise-ranking-model-evaluation**: Use when you have an unretrained pairwise ranking model (e.g., ROASMI ensemble) and one or more independent datasets with experimental elution orders, and need to quantify cross-dataset generalizability via rank score, accuracy, and Spearman correlation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-ranking-model-evaluation))
- **pairwise-recruiting-pipeline-review**: Review the pipeline of one Pairwise service request or job: how many candidates are sourced, contacted, interested and in evaluation, where evaluations or interviews stand, and what is waiting on someone. Use when the user asks "how is the &lt;role&gt; request (or job) going?", "who is in evaluation?", "who is interviewing?", "what is stuck?", or pastes a Pairwise pipeline link. Follows the pairwise-mcp skill for words and links. ([Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills](https://github.com/Pairwise-Technologies/pairwise-mcp-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pairwise-recruiting-pipeline-review) · ★4)
- **pairwise-registration**: Construct and smoke-test VoxelMorph VxmPairwise registration models with current PyTorch APIs, Neurite losses, checkpoint patterns, and legacy-script caveats. ([VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill](https://github.com/VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/repositories/repo-skills/voxelmorph/sub-skills/pairwise-registration) · ★357)
- **pairwise-result-set-comparison**: Use when you have top-hit annotation lists from multiple scorer/filter configurations (e.g. fingerprint, fragprint, totalscore scorers with element, formula, or InChI filters) and need to quantify whether they retrieve the same or distinct candidate subsets per spectrum. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-result-set-comparison))
- **pairwise-scaffold-distance-matrix-construction**: Use when you have a FASTA of predicted viral scaffolds and need a scaffold-by-scaffold nucleotide feature distance table (7 features per pair) to feed ML-based viral binning like vRhyme's. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-scaffold-distance-matrix-construction))
- **pairwise-self-verification-reasoning**: Verify solution quality through pairwise comparison rather than pointwise scoring. Implement topology coverage and Swiss refinement to allocate verification compute to uncertain pairs, improving calibration and reducing verification overhead. ([ADu2021/skillXiv](https://github.com/ADu2021/skillXiv/tree/HEAD/skills/skillxiv-v0.0.2-claude-opus-4.6/pairwise-self-verification-reasoning) · ★7)
- **pairwise-sequence-alignment-of-derived-fragments**: Use when small RNA-seq reads have been mapped to tRNA loci and you need to define the exact read-end/tRNA cleavage interface, classify tRNA halves vs tRF-5/tRF-3, or verify a conserved cleavage motif. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-sequence-alignment-of-derived-fragments))
- **pairwise-similarity-computation-scrna-seq**: Use when clustering scRNA-seq expression data whose high dropout and noise make naive two-cell similarity unreliable, and a robust cell-to-cell similarity matrix is needed as input to spectral clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-similarity-computation-scrna-seq))
- **pairwise-similarity-heatmap-visualization**: Use when several normalization outputs (e.g. tGAM, rGAM, rLOESS, QC-RLSC, SERFF, TIGER) of the same LC-MS metabolomics matrix must be compared, to visualize cross-method agreement via pairwise Spearman correlation heatmaps alongside DAM overlap percentages. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-similarity-heatmap-visualization))
- **pairwise-similarity-matrix-computation**: Use when you need to score every pair of MS/MS peak lists (m/z and intensity values with precursor metadata) against each other with entropy similarity instead of dot-product similarity, e.g. to build a library-search similarity matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-similarity-matrix-computation))
- **pairwise-similarity-matrix-construction**: Use when after XCMS feature detection and retention time correction, when you have a feature abundance table aligned across samples and need to group features that likely arise from the same compound before downstream annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-similarity-matrix-construction))
- **pairwise-similarity-network-construction**: Use when you need to cluster a moderate-sized set of annotated BGCs (e.g. 92 MIBiG v1.3 curated groups) into GCFs by exhaustive pairwise comparison, and need non-convex clusters that partitional Euclidean-space methods may miss. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-similarity-network-construction))
- **pairwise-similarity-scoring-weighted-metrics**: Use when after anchor selection and RT mapping spline fitting, when you have a fitted metabCombiner object with pre-aligned feature pair candidates and need to determine which parameter weights (A for RT, B for m/z, C for similarity score) discriminate true metabolite matches from false positives. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-similarity-scoring-weighted-metrics) · 2 copies)
- **pairwise-snp-indel-discovery-between-samples**: Use when two raw FASTA/FASTQ read collections from related samples must be compared for SNP/INDEL discovery without aligning to a reference genome, and sensitivity against a de Bruijn graph baseline (DISCOSNP++) must be quantified. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/pairwise-snp-indel-discovery-between-samples))
- **pairwise-spearman-correlation-across-methods**: Use when you have per-method regulator activity score matrices from decoupleR benchmarks and need to test whether different activity inference methods produce similar estimates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-spearman-correlation-across-methods))
- **pairwise-spectral-comparison**: Use when you have a collection of preprocessed mass spectra (in mzML, mzXML, msp, MGF, or JSON format) and need to quantify similarity relationships across all pairs—for instance, to identify redundant spectra in a library, cluster related compounds, or perform spectral library searches. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/pairwise-spectral-comparison) · 3 copies)
- **pairwise-spectral-overlap-quantification**: Use when you need pairwise similarity/distance between complete MS/MS datasets (e.g. for phylogenetics, QC, or species identification) without peptide identification or spectral libraries. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-spectral-overlap-quantification))
- **pairwise-spectral-similarity-matrix-analysis**: Use when exploring an MS/MS feature set via its pairwise spectral similarity matrix — e.g. you have a .mgf-derived specXplore session object and want threshold-tunable local network views, heatmaps, and clustering overlays rather than fixed molecular networking output. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-spectral-similarity-matrix-analysis))
- **pairwise-spectral-similarity-scoring**: Use when a set of MS/MS feature spectra (e.g. .mgf with feature_id, precursor m/z, m/z-intensity tuples) must be converted into pairwise similarity matrices to drive downstream embedding, network, or heatmap visualizations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pairwise-spectral-similarity-scoring))
- **pairwise-spectrum-comparison**: Use when you have a collection of cleaned mass spectrometry spectra (in mzML, mzXML, msp, MGF, or JSON format) and need to identify spectral similarities, find related compounds, or generate a comprehensive similarity matrix for downstream analysis such as spectral library matching or clustering.
